AP-75 Barriers to affordable housing – 91.220(j) 
Introduction:  
After consulting with more than 30 community stakeholders, non-profits, and public agencies, the City 
of Cheyenne has developed thoughtful and achievable actions to address the negative impacts of public 
policies that create barriers to affordable housing. Building on recommendations from the Mayor’s 
Affordable Housing Task Force, the City has made significant progress in updating the Unified 
Development Code to better support housing development. The Mayor, City Council, department 
directors, and staff recognize the challenges posed by the current housing shortage for Cheyenne’s 
residents and businesses. They remain committed to collaborating with partners to accelerate housing 
development and expand the supply of affordable units in the community. 
Actions it planned to remove or ameliorate the negative effects of public policies 
that serve as barriers to affordable housing such as land use controls, tax policies 
affecting land, zoning ordinances, building codes, fees and charges, growth 
limitations, and policies affecting the return on residential investment 
Below are the actions the City of Cheyenne has planned for the 2025 Program Year to remove or reduce 
the negative effects of public policies that serve as barriers to affordable housing, such as land use 
controls, tax policies affecting land, zoning ordinances, building codes, fees and charges, growth 
limitations, and policies affecting the return on residential investment: 
• 
Identify funding opportunities and provide technical support to affordable housing developers. 
• 
Provide updates on Cheyenne’s barriers to affordable housing development to state legislators 
and advocate for policies and funding that support affordable housing and economic 
development. 
• 
Explore strategies to streamline local permitting and zoning processes. 
• 
Develop public engagement tools to educate the community about city projects and initiatives, 
and to provide clear, consistent information to developers. 
• 
Allocate CDBG funds to support infrastructure and facility upgrades for transitional housing, as 
well as housing for seniors and vulnerable populations. 
• 
Participate in long-term planning meetings for affordable housing projects involving multiple 
stakeholders, with a focus on developing housing for vulnerable populations such as seniors and 
persons with disabilities. 
• 
Explore innovative collaborations between public and private affordable housing developers and 
municipalities that result in impactful affordable housing development projects like Hope 
Springs in Greely, CO. 
 
 

 
 
Annual Action Plan 
2025 
163 
OMB Control No: 2506-0117 (exp. 09/30/2021) 
AP-85 Other Actions – 91.220(k) 
Introduction:  
Each year, the Housing & Community Development (H&CD) Division works in partnership with the H&CD 
Advisory Council to assess community needs and establish funding priorities. This collaborative process 
includes public participation and consultation with local agencies to ensure that funding decisions reflect 
Cheyenne’s current priority needs and goals. The CDBG application cycle typically begins in October with 
information sessions, followed by a single round of applications accepted in November. Applications are 
reviewed using a standardized scoring system, and funding recommendations are made in January. 
Funds are awarded to subrecipients and become available the following September. 
 
For Program Year 2025, the H&CD Division is prioritizing projects that provide essential public services 
and infrastructure and public facility improvements. All funded activities for the 2025 Program Year are 
designed to benefit low- and moderate-income individuals or households.  
 
These funded activities leverage limited CDBG Entitlement funds with local resources, maximizing the 
positive impact on Cheyenne residents’ quality of life. 
 
Any unused funds from a program year are carried forward and added to the next funding cycle, 
ensuring that all available resources are fully utilized to address the city’s most pressing community 
development needs. 
Actions planned to address obstacles to meeting underserved needs 
The City plans to overcome obstacles to meeting the needs of underserved populations through a 
comprehensive, collaborative approach. All 2025 activities funded by the CDBG programs are 
intentionally designed to serve low- and moderate-income households, seniors, youth, victims of 
domestic violence, people experiencing homelessness, and other vulnerable groups. To maximize 
impact, the City is providing additional funding to activities that receive public service awards. 
Recognizing the federal 15% cap on public services, the City will contribute around $35,000 annually 
during the 2025–2027 Consolidated Planning period, using designated H&CD Housing Program funds. 
This supplemental funding will enable more organizations to access critical resources and expand 

 
 
Annual Action Plan 
2025 
164 
OMB Control No: 2506-0117 (exp. 09/30/2021) 
services to those most in need. 
 
The H&CD Division offers ongoing technical assistance to local nonprofits and agencies interested in 
applying for CDBG funds. This support continues throughout the grant management process, ensuring 
compliance with federal requirements and helping partner organizations build capacity. By assisting 
organizations in navigating regulatory requirements and overcoming administrative challenges, the City 
facilitates greater access to funding and encourages broader program participation. 
 
Strong partnerships with city departments, local nonprofits, and service organizations are integral to 
identifying needs, coordinating services, and developing strategies to meet the needs of underserved 
populations. H&CD staff actively participate in local initiatives such as the Point-In-Time Count, the 
Laramie County Community Partnership Housing Task Force, and the WyoNAHRO Board to ensure 
alignment of efforts and effective resource leveraging. Ongoing communication and consultation with a 
broad range of stakeholders-including organizations serving individuals with disabilities (such as Magic 
City, HealthWorks, and CALC), those supporting people experiencing homelessness (including 
Safehouse, Comea, Family Promise, Needs, and the Unaccompanied Student Initiative), and additional 
partners such as Recover Wyoming, Habitat for Humanity, Climb Wyoming, and Laramie County 
Community College (LCCC)-ensure that program planning and implementation remain responsive to 
evolving community needs. 
 
These coordinated actions reflect the City’s ongoing commitment to evaluating performance, adjusting 
strategies, and leveraging partnerships to remove obstacles to meeting unserved needs and improve 
outcomes for Cheyenne’s underserved residents. 
Actions planned to foster and maintain affordable housing 
The City is prioritizing the improvement and rehabilitation of public facilities, including transitional 
housing, housing for special needs populations, and shelters, in response to the growing demand for 
services that address homelessness and housing instability. For Program Year 2025 (PY25), the City has 
allocated $60,000 to enhance sidewalks and accessibility at the Community Action of Laramie County’s 
(CALC) Smith Manor Apartments. These transitional housing units support extremely low-income 
residents who are transitioning out of homelessness. In addition to managing the housing development, 
CALC provides Smith Manor residents with comprehensive wrap-around services to help them achieve 
self-sufficiency. 
The City will also open a Supplemental or Mini Grant Cycle, offering up to $259,000 for shovel-ready 
infrastructure and public facility projects that benefit low- and moderate-income (LMI) housing and 

 
 
Annual Action Plan 
2025 
165 
OMB Control No: 2506-0117 (exp. 09/30/2021) 
serve vulnerable populations. This additional grant cycle is made possible by reallocating funds returned 
by Habitat for Humanity of Laramie County, along with unspent funding from previous program years. 
The City is committed to the timely expenditure of federal funds and is excited to offer this bonus grant 
round to support much-needed improvements to facilities and infrastructure that impact LMI residents. 
To further support these initiatives, the City has allocated CDBG funds to engage environmental review 
consultants with expertise in HUD CEST-level and Environmental Assessment (EA) reviews. These 
consultants will prepare HEROS reviews for each project, ensuring compliance with all regulatory 
requirements. By outsourcing these specialized reviews, the H&CD staff can dedicate more time to 
community engagement and inter-departmental collaboration, further strengthening the City’s 
institutional capacity. By providing this support, the City hopes to encourage nonprofits and housing 
developers, who may have been hesitant to apply for CDBG funds due to environmental requirements, 
to participate in future grant cycles for housing rehabilitation and acquisition activities. 
Actions planned to reduce lead-based paint hazards 
H&CD Division policies require all HUD-funded projects to comply with federal standards for testing and 
remediation. Additionally, subrecipients must provide EPA Renovate Right information and actively 
educate clients and beneficiaries about the dangers of lead exposure and the importance of 
remediation. The City’s Economic Resource Administrator and Community Development Manager plan 
to research potential federal funding for service line replacement, as well as other funding opportunities 
to establish a program that would replace lead lines for low- and moderate-income (LMI) homeowners. 
If funding sources are identified, the City will collaborate with the Cheyenne Board of Public Utility on 
this project. 
 
 

 
 
Annual Action Plan 
2025 
166 
OMB Control No: 2506-0117 (exp. 09/30/2021) 
 
Actions planned to reduce the number of poverty-level families 
The City’s plans to reduce the number of poverty-level families through providing employment training, 
digital literacy, support for survivors of domestic violence, and food security for seniors. 
Climb Wyoming Employment Training 
CDBG funds will continue to support Climb Wyoming, a statewide nonprofit that provides free, industry-
specific job training and career placement for low- and moderate-income single mothers. The program’s 
holistic approach includes not only technical and job skills training, but also mental health counseling 
and wraparound services such as childcare, transportation, and housing assistance. By equipping 
participants with marketable skills and supporting their transition into stable employment, Climb 
Wyoming directly addresses the root causes of poverty among single-parent families and helps break 
cycles of generational poverty. 
Laramie County Community College (LCCC) ACES Program 
CDBG funds have been allocated to provide free digital literacy and workforce development training. 
The Laramie County Community College Adult Career & Education Services (ACES) program offers free 
courses in basic literacy, high school equivalency preparation, English as a Second Language, and digital 
skills training. These programs are designed to help participants build foundational and advanced skills 
needed for higher-wage jobs in a digital economy, supporting long-term income growth and social 
mobility. 
Shelter Utility Reimbursement  
CDBG allocations will help cover essential operating costs for local domestic violence shelters. This 
shelter utility reimbursement support is critical for providing shelter that empowers survivors of 
domestic violence to achieve self-sufficiency. 
Meals on Wheels and Food Pantry Support 
The city directs CDBG funds to Meals on Wheels and local food pantries, ensuring that seniors and other 
low- and moderate-income residents have reliable access to nutritious food. These services help reduce 
the financial burden of grocery expenses, prevent malnutrition among seniors on fixed incomes, and 
support overall household stability. 
 
Collectively, these initiatives represent a coordinated effort to address both the immediate needs and 
the underlying barriers faced by families experiencing poverty, with a focus on sustainable employment, 

 
 
Annual Action Plan 
2025 
167 
OMB Control No: 2506-0117 (exp. 09/30/2021) 
digital inclusion, housing stability, and food security. 
Actions planned to develop institutional structure  
The City of Cheyenne is committed to strengthening its institutional framework to more effectively 
deliver federal funds and support Wyoming residents. The City collaborates closely with internal staff 
and departments to continuously refine its delivery systems for federal programs, ensuring compliance 
and maximizing community impact. 
Regional Collaboration and Best Practices 
• H&CD Division staff actively participate in the HUD Region 8 User Group, a network of professionals 
from jurisdictions receiving HUD funding. 
• This group facilitates the exchange of best practices and innovative strategies for managing federal

areas such as Davis-Bacon certified payroll review, Build America, Buy America (BABA), and Section 3 
compliance. 
• These trainings are designed to enhance the City’s capacity to meet federal requirements and ensure

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION DIRECTING THAT PLANS FOR THE 15TH STREET 
CORRIDOR

PROJECT 
PRESERVE 
THE

GUNSLINGERS’ 
CURRENT 
LOCATION, 
LAYOUT, 
BUILDINGS AND LANDSCAPING AT WEST 15TH STREET AND

WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Gunslingers are situated at Gunslinger Square located on West 
15th Street and Pioneer Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and represent the traditional Western 
history of the City, providing entertainment to the Downtown district; 
 
WHEREAS, the 15th Street Corridor Revitalization Project (15th Street Project), as 
presented during the January 31st, 2025, Work Session of the City Council indicated that the 
Cheyenne Gunslingers and Gunslinger Square would remain on 15th Street but move from the 
present location; 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Gunslingers’ mission is to bring citizens and visitors to the 
Downtown district during the months of June and July along with providing additional foot traffic 
to Downtown businesses; 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Gunslingers are also important during Cheyenne Frontier Days 
to attract citizens and visitors to the Downtown district during the afternoon and evening hours; 
 
WHEREAS, the Gunslinger Square attracts citizens and visitors year-round and maintains 
the City’s traditional Western history; and 
 
WHEREAS, during the Christmas Season the Gunslinger Square is decorated for the Old 
West Holiday by Visit Cheyenne.  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Cheyenne Gunslingers and Gunslinger Square shall 
remain at their present location on West 15th Street and Pioneer Avenue in Cheyenne, Wyoming 
with their present landscaping, layout and buildings; and  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that City staff take appropriate steps to enable the 
Cheyenne Gunslingers to remain as described above. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
10:48 am, Jun 17 2025

2025.

MINIMUM REVENUE GUARANTEED PAYMENT AGREEMENT 
Between 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, AND 
CHEYENNE REGIONAL AIR SERVICE FOCUS TEAM (CRAFT) 
 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into by and between the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, a municipal corporation organized under the laws of the State of Wyoming, 2101 O'Neil 
Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 ("CITY"), and the Cheyenne Regional Air Service Focus 
Team, c/o Cheyenne LEADS, P.O. Box 1045, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82003-1045 ("CRAFT"). The 
CITY and CRAFT are sometimes individually referred to herein as “Party” and collectively as 
“Parties.” The Parties agree as follows: 
 
I.   PURPOSE 
 
 
The purpose of this Agreement is for CITY to pay a portion of the Minimum Revenue 
Guaranteed ("MRG") funds to CRAFT, for payments to SkyWest Airlines for providing scheduled 
flight services between Cheyenne, Wyoming, and Denver, Colorado. 
 
II.   TERM 
 
 
This Agreement shall commence on the date last executed by the duly authorized 
representatives of the Parties to this Agreement and shall remain in full force and effect until June 
30, 2026, or until terminated as provided herein. 
 
III.  RESPONSIBILITIES OF CITY 
 
 
The CITY shall pay to CRAFT up to two hundred fifty-three thousand five hundred 
seventy-two dollars ($253,572.00) of the MRG, which MRG is capped at one million two hundred 
and sixty-seven thousand eight hundred and fifty-seven dollars ($1,267,857.00), upon presentation 
of a properly executed invoice and accounting as prescribed by the City Treasurer. The CITY shall 
be responsible for twenty percent (20%) of the MRG amounts billed by SkyWest Airlines. The 
CITY’s payment obligation under this Agreement is capped at two hundred fifty-three thousand 
five hundred seventy-two dollars ($253,572.00). The Parties understand and agree that any MRG 
amount that is payable to SkyWest Airlines only comes into effect if, and when, SkyWest Airlines 
fails to meet the MRG margin. Payments shall be made in accordance with Wyo. Stat. § 16-6-602 
(as amended). 
 
IV.  RESPONSIBILITIES OF CRAFT 
 
 
A. 
CRAFT agrees that the funds paid by the CITY pursuant to this Agreement shall 
not be for the express aid of any private citizen, or in violation of Article 16, Section 6, of the 
Constitution of the State of Wyoming. 
 
B. 
CRAFT agrees to provide an invoice to the CITY consisting at a minimum of 
detailed accounting describing the costs and services provided pursuant to this Agreement. 
2:14 pm, May 27, 2025

 
Minimum Revenue Guaranteed Payment Agreement Between City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and 
Cheyenne Regional Air Service Focus Team (CRAFT)  
 
Page 2 of 5 
 
C. 
CITY acknowledges and agrees that CRAFT will not have an active role, other than 
a right to review and audit records, in the accounting of the costs associated with the services 
provided by SkyWest Airlines. Subject to the foregoing, for those funds passing through CRAFT, 
CRAFT agrees to maintain generally accepted accounting procedures and practices, and to 
maintain books, records, documents, and other evidence to sufficiently and properly reflect all 
transactions of any nature relating to this Agreement or any funds provided by or through the City 
of Cheyenne including, but not limited to, the funds referenced herein. Any books, records, 
documents, and other evidence shall be made available upon written request of the CITY for 
inspection at the offices of CRAFT in Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
 
D. 
CRAFT agrees to retain all required records for three (3) years after the CITY 
makes final payment and all other matters relating to the Agreement are concluded. CRAFT agrees 
to permit access by the CITY or any of its duly authorized representatives to any books, documents, 
papers, and records of CRAFT that are directly pertinent to this Agreement for purposes including, 
but not limited to, audit, examination, excerpts, and transcriptions. It is agreed that finished or 
unfinished documents, data, or reports, prepared by CRAFT under this Agreement shall be 
considered the property of the CITY and upon completion of the services to be performed, or upon 
termination of this Agreement for cause, or for the convenience of the CITY, will be turned over 
to the CITY. 
 
V.   GENERAL PROVISIONS 
 
 
A. 
Independent Contractor.  The services to be performed by CRAFT are those of an 
independent Contractor and not as an employee of the CITY. CRAFT is not eligible for City of 
Cheyenne employee benefits and will be treated as an independent contractor for federal tax filing 
purposes. CRAFT assumes responsibility for its personnel who provide services pursuant to this 
Agreement and will make all deductions required of employers by state, federal, and local laws 
and shall maintain liability insurance for each of them. CRAFT is free to perform the same or 
similar services for others. 
 
 
B. 
Acceptance Not Waiver.  The CITY’s approval of the reports and work or materials 
furnished hereunder shall not in any way relieve CRAFT of responsibility for the technical 
accuracy of the work. The CITY’s approval or acceptance of, or payment for, any of the services 
shall not be construed to operate as a waiver of any rights under this Agreement or of any cause of 
action arising out of the performance of this Agreement. 
 
 
C. 
Termination.  This Agreement may be terminated: (a) By either party at any time 
for failure of the other party to comply with the terms and conditions of this Agreement; (b) By 
either party with thirty (30) days' prior written notice to the other party; or (c) Upon mutual written 
agreement by both parties.

Cheyenne Regional Air Service Focus Team (CRAFT)

D. 
Entire Agreement.  This Agreement, consisting of five (5) pages, represents the 
entire and integrated agreement and understanding between the parties and supersedes all prior 
negotiations, statements, representations, and agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
 
E. 
Assignment.  Neither this Agreement nor any rights or obligations hereunder shall 
be assigned or delegated by a party without the prior written consent of the other party. 
 
 
F. 
Modification.  This Agreement shall be modified only by a written agreement, duly 
executed by all parties hereto. 
 
 
G. 
Invalidity.  If any provision of this Agreement is held invalid or unenforceable by 
any court of competent jurisdiction, or if the CITY is advised of any such actual or potential 
invalidity or unenforceability, such holding, or advice shall not invalidate or render unenforceable 
any other provision hereof. It is the express intent of the parties that the provisions of this 
Agreement are fully severable. 
 
 
H. 
Applicable Law and Venue.  The parties mutually understand and agree this 
Agreement shall be governed by and interpreted pursuant to the laws of the State of Wyoming, 
without regard to its conflict of laws principles. If any dispute arises between the parties from or 
concerning this Agreement or the subject matter hereof, any suit or proceeding at law or in equity 
shall be brought in the District Court of the State of Wyoming, First Judicial District, sitting at 
Cheyenne, Wyoming. The foregoing provisions of this paragraph are agreed by the parties to be a 
material inducement to CRAFT and to the CITY in executing this Agreement. This provision is 
not intended, nor shall it be construed to waive the CITY’s governmental immunity as provided in 
this Agreement. 
 
 
I. 
Contingencies.  CRAFT certifies and warrants no gratuities, kickbacks, or 
contingency fees were paid in connection with this Agreement, nor were any fees, commissions, 
gifts, or other considerations made contingent upon the award of this Agreement. 
 
 
J. 
Discrimination.  All parties agree they will not discriminate against any person who 
performs work under the terms and conditions of this Agreement because of race, color, gender, 
creed, handicapping condition, or national origin. 
 
 
K. 
ADA Compliance.  All parties agree they will not discriminate against a qualified 
individual with disability, pursuant to a law as set forth in the Americans With Disabilities Act, 
P.L. 101-336, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and/or any properly promulgated rules and regulations 
relating thereto. 
 
 
L. 
Governmental Immunity:  The CITY does not waive its Governmental Immunity, 
as provided by any applicable law, including Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., by entering into this 

 
Minimum Revenue Guaranteed Payment Agreement Between City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and 
Cheyenne Regional Air Service Focus Team (CRAFT)  
 
Page 4 of 5 
Agreement. Further, the CITY fully retains all immunities and defenses provided by law regarding 
any action, whether in tort, contract, or any other theory of law, based on this Agreement. 
 
 
M. 
Indemnification.  To the fullest extent permitted by law, CRAFT agrees to 
indemnify and hold harmless the CITY, its elected and appointed officials, employees, and 
volunteers from any and all liability for injuries, damages, claims, penalties, actions, demands, or 
expenses arising solely from or in connection with the work that is to be performed by or on behalf 
of CRAFT for the CITY except to the extent liability is caused by the sole negligence or willful 
misconduct of the CITY, its elected and appointed officials, employees, and volunteers. CRAFT 
shall carry liability insurance sufficient to cover its obligations under this provision and provide 
CITY with proof of such insurance. 
 
 
N. 
Third Parties.  The parties do not intend to create in any other individual or entity 
the status of third-party beneficiary, and this Agreement shall not be construed to create such 
status. The rights, duties, and obligations contained in this Agreement shall operate only between 
the parties to the Agreement and shall inure solely to the benefit of the parties to this Agreement.  
 
 
O. 
Conflict of Interest.  The CITY and CRAFT affirm, to their knowledge, no CRAFT 
employee has any personal beneficial interest whatsoever in the Agreement described herein. No 
staff member of CRAFT, compensated either partially or wholly with funds from this Agreement, 
shall engage in any conduct or activity which would constitute a conflict of interest relative to this 
Agreement. 
 
 
P. 
Force Majeure.  The performance of the Agreement by either party shall be subject 
to force majeure including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural disaster, war or threat 
of war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental regulation 
or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by the World Health Organization, the Centers 
for Disease Control, or local government authority or health agencies (including, but not limited

by either party, without liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or amounts 
paid shall be refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written notice to the other

availability of funds which are appropriated, allocated, or approved by the Governing Body for the 
payment of this obligation. If funds are not allocated, appropriated, or approved and available, in 
the fiscal judgment of the CITY, for the continuance of the services and equipment provided by

to have been given, when deposited in the U.S. Mail, properly stamped and addressed to the party 
for whom intended at such party's address listed herein, or when delivered personally to such party.

INDUSTRIAL AND ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT SERVICES AGREEMENT 
BETWEEN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND THE CHEYENNE/LARAMIE 
COUNTY CORPORATION FOR ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT 
 
1. 
Parties. The parties to this Agreement are the City of Cheyenne, a municipal corporation 
organized under the laws of the State of Wyoming, whose address is 2101 0'Neil Avenue, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, hereinafter ("City"), and the Cheyenne/Laramie County Corporation 
for Economic Development, whose address is 121 West 15th Street, Suite 304, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming 82001, hereinafter ("LEADS"). 
 
2. 
Purpose of Agreement. The purpose of this Agreement is to authorize LEADS to promote 
and take advantage of marketing opportunities to advertise the City's resources and to enhance the 
industrial development and economic viability of the city of Cheyenne. 
 
3. 
Term. This Agreement is effective when all parties have executed it and all approvals have 
been granted. The term of this Agreement shall be from July 1, 2025, through June 30, 2026.  
 
4. 
Payment. The City agrees to pay LEADS for industrial and economic development 
services in the amount of one hundred thousand dollars ($100,000.00). Payment will be made in a 
lump sum on or before December 31, 2025. The City’s obligation to make payment to LEADS 
under this Agreement is subject to the appropriation of funds for this purpose through the City's 
budget approval process. 
 
5. 
LEADS Responsibilities. LEADS will: 
 
a. 
Encourage industrial and economic development in the City of Cheyenne through 
activities designed to increase infrastructure and basic employment opportunities, and to enhance 
the City's quality of life by attracting and retaining organizations compatible with the City of 
Cheyenne's environment. 
 
b. 
Promote and take advantage of marketing opportunities to enhance the industrial 
development and economic viability of the city of Cheyenne. 
 
c. 
Maintain generally accepted accounting procedures and practices, and to maintain 
books, records, documents and other evidence to sufficiently and properly reflect all transactions 
of any nature relating to this Agreement. Such books, records, documents, and other evidence shall 
be made available upon written request of the City Treasurer for inspection at the Cheyenne 
LEADS office. 
 
d. 
Provide an annual written report documenting the uses of funds received under this 
Agreement. The report will be provided to the Mayor or his designee no less than ninety (90) days 
before the end of the term of this Agreement. 
 
2:19 pm, May 27, 2025

General Provisions. 
 
a. 
Amendments. Any changes, modifications, revisions or amendments to this 
Agreement which are mutually agreed upon by the parties to this Agreement shall be incorporated 
by written instrument, executed and signed by all parties to this Agreement. 
 
b. 
Applicable Law/Choice of Venue. The construction, interpretation and 
enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming. The courts 
of the State of Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over this Agreement and the parties, and the venue 
shall be in the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
c. 
Compliance with Laws. LEADS shall keep informed of and comply with all 
applicable federal, state and local laws and regulations in the performance of this Agreement. 
 
d. 
Entirety of Agreement. This Agreement consisting of three (3) pages represents the 
entire and integrated Agreement between the parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, 
representations and agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
e. 
Indemnification. LEADS shall release, indemnify and hold harmless the City of 
Cheyenne and their officers, agents, employees, successors and assigns from any cause of action, 
or claims or demands arising out of LEADS’ performance under this Agreement. 
 
f. 
Independent Contractor. LEADS shall function as an independent contractor for the 
purposes of this Agreement. LEADS shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities 
that may be incurred by LEADS. Nothing in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing 
LEADS or its agents or employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City 
or to incur any obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
g. 
Notice. All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this Agreement shall be 
in writing and given to the parties either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
 
h. 
Nondiscrimination. LEADS shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the 
Wyoming Fair Employment Practices Act, Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq., and the Americans with 
Disabilities Act (ADA), 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq. LEADS shall assure that no person is 
discriminated against based on the grounds of age, race, sex, creed, color, national origin, ancestry, 
qualified disability, or pregnancy in connection with the performance of this Agreement. 
 
i. 
Prior Approval. This Agreement shall not be binding upon either party, no services 
shall be performed under the terms of this Agreement, nor shall payment be made until this 
Agreement has been reduced to writing and approved by all necessary authorities.

MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Wesley D. Bay, P.E., City Drainage Engineer 
 
DATE: 
June 4, 2025 
 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Service Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and HDR 
Engineering, Inc., for the 18th Street Reconstruction Project.  
 
 
Please find the attached Professional Service Agreement between the City and HDR Engineering, 
Inc., for the 18th Street Reconstruction Project.   
 
The project is located along 18th Street Between Snyder Avenue and House Avenue. The project 
consists of the design of a storm sewer interceptor to protect the historic downtown area from 
flooding, water transmission system design, sanitary sewer component replacement, ADA 
upgrades bringing this section of 18th Street into full ADA compliance, roadway design, and 
streetscape design.   
 
Engineering Staff evaluates fee proposals for engineering services based on time and material 
estimates for the specific project.  Those costs are readily tracked and verified during design 
operations and the City only pays for the actual time expended. We believe this agreement will 
provide the required services to complete analysis and preliminary design portion of the project. 
 
Funding for this project will come from the 23 – 26 Optional 1% Tax Fund (5th Penny), a Federal 
Emergency Management Agency (FEMA) Hazard Mitigation Grant Program (HMGP) grant and 
the Board of Public Utility. The total professional services will be a cost not-to-exceed 
$1,100,562.00. We respectfully request your approval of this professional services agreement. 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
CITY ENGINEER’S OFFICE 
2101 O’Neil Avenue  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 

 
Page 1 of 8 
City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
18th Street Reconstruction Project 
 
This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and HDR Engineering, Inc., located at 7350 Stockman Street, Suite A, Cheyenne, WY 
82009 (“Consultant”). This Agreement shall become effective upon the date of the last signature 
affixed hereto. 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, in 
accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by reference 
and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work extends until the work described in Exhibit A is 
completed. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the Consultant 
will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount of One 
Million One Hundred Thousand Five Hundred Sixty-Two Dollars ($1,100,562.00) as 
stated in Exhibit A. 
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the Consultant, 
the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services not meeting 
such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in 
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
4:05 pm, Jun 03, 2025

 
Page 2 of 8 
6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of Cheyenne 
has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to alcohol and 
drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other consultants under 
contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-consultants, are required to 
comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy for drug 
and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites occupied by the Consultant while 
performing the duties and responsibilities of the Agreement. It is the responsibility of the 
Consultant to familiarize themselves with the requirements of this policy and to inform all 
their employees and sub-consultants of the requirements and ensure their compliance. If 
the Consultant, their employees or sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the 
Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a result 
the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any person, 
destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations, or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or 
sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement. The Consultant acknowledges that 
it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 per 
occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement. Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect from any and 
all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties under this 
Agreement. The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be provided pursuant to 
an “occurrence” policy. 

 
Page 3 of 8 
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, the 
Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City with a 
new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be 
in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services under 
this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., 
b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City advance 
written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall provide the 
City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period (“tail coverage”) 
which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than five (5) years 
following the last date that Consultant has provided services under this 
Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements. This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and professional 
liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the above-
stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All policies 
required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement. It shall 
be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify in writing the city risk 
manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that has 
resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change to any insurance 
coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach 
of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured. The City’s 
failure to request or review such endorsements or certificates shall not affect the City’s 
rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 

 
Page 4 of 8 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national

10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a written

11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT

TAXES

13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default. In the event the default remains 
uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution through the completion of 
services. The parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement.

The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. Upon

a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c.

for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of termination. 
Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld.

ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the

17.

GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its

the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement. Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses

19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are

and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in progress; to 
examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this Agreement;

24. 
SUPPLEMENTAL GENERAL CONDITIONS

agreed by and between the parties as follows:

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
Project Name:  18th Street Reconstruction Project 
 
Page 2 of 8 
 
A.1 Overview 
 
Consultant services for the 18th Street Reconstruction Project are the design and development of a complete 
set of construction documents, including plans, specifications, and estimates. Any innovative solutions that 
are cost effective and meet project goals will be incorporated during project scoping negotiations and 
included in the design.  Work required for this project will include project management; hydrologic and 
hydraulic analysis; storm and sanitary sewer design; water transmission system design; roadway design; 
geotechnical investigation; right-of-way surveying; and utility location, coordination, and design. 
The project requires a professional design team that includes a Civil Engineer licensed in the State of 
Wyoming with a minimum of 10 years of project design experience in one or more of the following 
disciplines: storm and sanitary sewer design; hydrologic and hydraulic analysis; water transmission system 
design; roadway design; with the ability to provide in-house or capacity to sub-consult for survey services, 
geotechnical investigation and those disciplines required for the project.  
 
A.2 Project Description 
 
A.2.a Overview 
 
The project is located along 18th Street and extends from House Avenue west to Snyder Avenue, or 
potentially Crow Creek, as vetted through the storm sewer design. The 18th Street Reconstruction project is 
comprised of roadway design, storm sewer design, sanitary sewer design, and water transmission system 
design. 
The project will include the design of a storm sewer interceptor mainline and inlets along 18th Street. The 
outfall may be done via a tie-in with the existing 19th Street 60” steel reinforced polyethylene (SRPE) 
interceptor pipe and/or extend to Crow Creek under 18th Street. This will require determining if the 19th 
storm main has sufficient capacity to take on storm water from the proposed 18th Street system. Included in 
the hydrologic and hydraulic analysis of this portion of the Capitol Basin will be the evaluation of additional 
storm infrastructure that has been constructed within the last decade. These systems include the 26th Street 
Interceptor, Civic Commons storm system and detention pond, and the 19th Street Interceptor. 
This project also includes the replacement of the existing water main from Snyder Ave to House Ave 
including a new casing under the Reed Ave railroad tracks. The new water main will be relocated to the 
typical ROW alignment where possible and eliminate parallel mains.  The replacement will include all 
branch water mains, fire hydrant leads, fire hydrants, and water service lines within the ROW.  This project 
also includes the replacement of the existing manholes and lampholes in the ROW. 
A.2.b Project History and Existing Facilities 
 
18th Street within the proposed project limits is a minor collector. The 18th Street roadway corridor has a 
multitude of both wet and dry utilities, most of which are in need of replacement. There is insufficient storm 
sewer to protect the central and eastern downtown area during significant storm events.. The cast iron water 
main in the corridor dates from the 1940’s to 1960’s and is corroded and undersized for adequate water 

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
Project Name:  18th Street Reconstruction Project 
 
Page 3 of 8 
 
pressure and volume for fire protection. The water lines will need to be adjusted to accommodate the 
increased storm sewer main size and inlet laterals. 
A.2.d Proposed Improvements 
 
Proposed improvements include roadway reconstruction, storm sewer construction, sanitary sewer 
upgrades, water transmission system upgrades, ADA upgrades, and utility relocation.  
At a minimum, the following should be expected as tasks for the project: 
• 
Roadway geometric design. 
o Vertical and horizontal alignment of 18th Street. 
 
Establishment of a tie between roadway and wet utilities components. 
 
Constraints from existing buildings, intersections, and overall area topography 
will allow for only minimal changes to the vertical and horizontal alignments of 
18th Street. 
 
ADA facility upgrades in the ROW. 
• 
Storm sewer design 
o Hydrologic and hydraulic evaluation  
 
Evaluation of added infrastructure to the west and central portions of the Capitol 
Drainage Basin. 
o Interceptor sizing, inlet locations, and associated elevations. 
• 
Potable water transmission system design. 
o Mains, fire hydrant leads, fire hydrants, and service lines in the ROW. 
• 
Sanitary sewer design. 
o Manhole and lamphole replacement in the ROW. 
A.2.e Right-Of-Way 
 
Right-of-way acquisition is not anticipated for this project.  
 
A.2.f Project Funding and Timing 
 
There is sufficient funding allocated toward the design of the project. The storm sewer design will be 
partially funded by a FEMA grant. Roadway design will be funded by the 5th Penny Optional Sales tax. 
Sanitary sewer wand water transmission design will be funded by the Board of Public Utilities (BOPU). 
This will require invoicing breakouts of each of the three funding mechanisms for design services and bid 
items. 
The design phase of the project is anticipated to be completed within fourteen (14) months from the date 
of execution of a Professional Services Agreement with the selected firm, or by August 1st, 2026. 
The construction of the project will be postponed until sufficient funding is available. An additional RFP 
will be released for construction management and/or engineering assistance at that time.

A.4 Environmental Clearances 
 
Environmental clearances are not anticipated for the project. 
 
A.5 Public Involvement 
 
Three public meetings will be held for the project.  The Consultant will provide public notice and media 
releases with assistance from the city; coordinate the venue of the meeting; prepare exhibits, handouts, and 
comment cards; and correlate public comments. A preliminary public meeting will be held to gain public 

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
Project Name:  18th Street Reconstruction Project 
 
Page 5 of 8 
 
input on the preferred intersection and roadway configuration. Two additional meetings will be held after 
the 30% and 60% plan issuances. 
 
A.6 General Design 
 
A.6.a Design and Construction Standards 
 
All design and construction will be done in accordance with the latest edition of the applicable standards 
including, but not limited to, the following: 
• 
AASHTO Policy on Geometric Design of Highways and Streets. 
• 
AASHTO LRFD Bridge Design Specifications. 
• 
AASHTO Roadside Design Guide 
• 
AASHTO Guide for the Development of Bicycle Facilities 
• 
Manual on Uniform Traffic Control Devices. 
• 
City of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities Standards and Specifications. 
• 
WYDOT Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Construction (structure only). 
• 
City of Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC). 
• 
Applicable railroad standards if in railroad ROW. 
 
Conflicts between design and construction standards will be resolved by the City. 
 
A.6.b Surveying 
 
Right-of-way surveying will be completed for the extent of the project as needed to complete the project 
design. Project control points will be established as needed. All surveying, design, and temporary control 
points will be referenced in the City control network.  Property corners necessary to establish the right-of-
way will be monumented as necessary. Existing right-of-way, any required permanent and temporary 
easements, and parcel ownership will be shown in the project plan sheets.  
 
A.6.c Utilities 
 
All known utilities will be investigated and incorporated into the project design. These include, at a 
minimum: 
• 
Telephone. 
• 
Internet and cable. 
• 
Electric and gas. 
• 
Water and sanitary sewer. 
• 
Signals, signs, and storm sewer. 
• 
Fiber optic cable. 
Any conflicts with other utilities, either active or abandoned, will be investigated and resolved.  
Preliminary construction sequencing/phasing and traffic routing plans along with special provisions will 
be developed to facilitate utility work. Locates will be performed for any potential utility conflicts, this 

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
Project Name:  18th Street Reconstruction Project 
 
Page 6 of 8 
 
includes both surface utility locates and non-destructive subsurface utility excavation locates. All utilities 
within the project limits will be shown on the project plan sheets.  
 
A.6.d Drainage Impact Study 
 
A Drainage Impact Study will be performed for the project area in accordance with Section 3.2 of the 
Unified Development Code. The report will document the drainage impacts (if any), both upstream and 
downstream, of the project and present a design to mitigate any negative impacts.  All supporting 
calculations will be included in the report. 
 
A.6.e Geotechnical Investigation 
 
A geotechnical investigation will be performed within the project limits and will include as necessary, but 
not be limited to: 
• 
Geological strata defining soil and/or bedrock locations and elevations. 
• 
Soil and/or rock properties. 
• 
Soil classification. 
• 
Recommended retaining wall and foundation types for any structures on the project (LRFD 
values). 
• 
Subsurface drainage characteristics. 
• 
Groundwater location. 
• 
Any other characteristics not explicitly stated herein but required for completion of the project. 
The results of the geological investigation and recommendations will be summarized in a geotechnical 
report. All required data, testing, bore hole locations, results, and recommendations, for the afore-
mentioned investigations, will be summarized and included in the report.  
 
A.6.f Surfacing Recommendations 
 
Surfacing recommendations will be prepared based on geotechnical investigations performed throughout 
the project limits. The recommendations will outline surfacing thicknesses and recommended materials 
for subbase, base, and finished grade courses. Surfacing recommendations, material types, mix designs, 
and associated rates of application will be summarized in a pavement design report. All material types 
and rates of application will be included in the design plan summaries.    
 
A.7 Plan Submittals 
 
A.7.a General 
 
Requirements for each formal plan issuance are outlined below and will act as a general guideline for 
submittals. All plan issuances will show utilities on plan and profile sheets. Additional intermediate and 
informal plan issuances may be required due any unforeseen issues encountered during the design 

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT

process. It should be expected that there will be two rounds of reviews and one plan inspection meeting 
for each plan issuance.  
 
A.7.b Preliminary Plans 
 
Preliminary plans will be submitted illustrating the conceptual roadway alignment, wet utility alignments, 
and proposed utility relocation. These plans should be approximately 30% complete. The general layout 
of the roadway, intersections/ADA, and wet utilities be clear and assist in the visualization and 
verification of the recommended design parameters and configurations. At a minimum, this plan submittal 
will include the following design elements: 
• 
Roadway alignments. 
• 
Preliminary storm sewer, sanitary sewer, and water transmission system alignments. 
• 
Preliminary earthwork. 
• 
Alternative design options that remain undecided. 
• 
Preliminary Engineer’s opinion of probable cost. 
 
A.7.c 60% Plans 
 
60% plans will be submitted containing the general layout of the roadway, intersection, and structures. 
These elements should be nearly finalized with only minor adjustments needed for utilities and right-of-
way. At a minimum, the plans will include the following design elements: 
• 
Vertical and horizontal roadway alignments with plan and profile sheets. 
• 
Roadway typical sections 
• 
Vertical and horizontal alignments for storm sewer, sanitary sewer, and water transmission 
system alignments with plan and profile sheets. 
• 
Construction easements. 
• 
Intersection/ADA layouts. 
• 
Earthwork. 
• 
Estimated quantities. 
• 
Engineer’s opinion of probable costs. 
 
A.7.d 90% Plans 
 
90% plans will be submitted when all right-of-way and utilities have been delineated and proposed 
relocations (if required) are finalized. All design elements required for the previous submittal will be 
finalized and incorporated into this plan set. This submittal will contain a complete set of construction 
documents, including plans, specifications, and estimates. This set of plans will contain the following: 
• 
Title sheet. 
• 
Legend sheet. 
• 
Summary sheets, using City bid items, which will include: 
o Individual summaries for all pay items. 
o Total Estimated Quantities summary. 
• 
Roadway typical sections.

Task 2.8 – Baseline Hydrologic & Hydraulic Analysis & Drainage Impact Study  
• 
HDR will work within the baseline model that has been established for the Capitol Basin area to 
understand existing conditions and begin to develop improvements and sizing for new 
infrastructure.  
o 
Review existing model and understand model logic and linkages.  
o 
Update based on available information from preceding consultant updates and 
improvements that have been made to the local drainage system.  
o 
Assess and develop scenarios based upon UDC criteria & as constructed drawings  
• 
Model Review and Updates 
o 
HDR will apply industry standard practices in modelling to perform a comprehensive 
review and update of the existing model.  
o 
Recommendations will be made for model improvements and implementation.  
• 
HDR will provide an updated conditions model for the 18th street updates along with the Drainage 
Impact Study for the City’s review. This study will comply with Section 3.2 of the UDC and will 
assess the following: 
o 
Upstream and downstream impacts 
o 
Compliance with drainage criteria for storm sewer design.  
o 
Preliminary Drainage Plan will be provided at this stage.  
o 
Define minimum project goals 
o 
Define issues/opportunities/fatal flaws that drive the concept plan (project goals, utility 
conflicts, grade, ROW/easements, etc). 
o 
Review the Existing model 
o 
Update the existing model for recent projects 
o 
Develop concept design alternative(s) based on input from the City and the results of the 
issues/opportunities/fatal flaw analysis 
 
Task 2.9 – Utility Design & Coordination (BOPU) 
 
• 
HDR will conduct preliminary utility design for replacement of the existing potable water system & 
water services within the project area, proposed storm sewer interceptor and inlets as determined 
by the hydrologic & hydraulic analysis and for replacement sanitary sewer manholes within the 
project area. 
o 
The design team will coordinate with Cheyenne BOPU for design of replacement of water 
mains, fire hydrants & fire hydrant leads and service lines within the ROW of the project 
area. 
o 
The design team will provide preliminary storm sewer design including storm drain 
interceptor alignment and profile and preliminary placement of inlets. This will be based 
off of the model updates that will be performed in the preceding tasks. Model sizing will 
dictate initial design criteria and will form part of the iterative process for enhancing the 
drainage design for this project. This phase will include coordination with the City’s 
Stormwater Division Manager. Preliminary storm sewer sizing will be back tested using 
the updated model to review system performance.  
o 
The design team will coordinate with Cheyenne BOPU for design of replacement sanitary 
sewer manholes within the ROW of the project area.  It is assumed that recent sewer 
CCTV inspection video will be provided to HDR for review and identification of potential 
sewer main defects that should be corrected prior to future CIPP lining planned by 
BOPU.  HDR will provide recommendations for sewer main replacements. 
• 
 Utility Deliverables include: 
o 
Storm Drain Alignment and Profile 
o 
Water Alignment and Profile 
o 
Sanitary Sewer Manhole Replacement Recommendations 
 
 

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming | 18th Street Reconstruction Project
Scope of Services – Rev.2 | May 2025
 
5 
 
Task 2.10 – 60% Plans   
 
• 
HDR will provide 60%Plans as requested in the RFP.  This includes plan and profile sheets with 
limited quantities for review by City 
o 
Full quantity take-offs will not be completed during this stage of review but shall be 
furthered from the Preliminary Plans Submittal 
o 
Cross Sections will not be completed for this stage of review 
• 
Project total quantities will be estimated to prepare a preliminary cost estimate 
o 
HDR will follow the American Association of Cost Estimators guidelines during this effort 
• 
Bid items will be based on Technical Specifications (City of Cheyenne & WYDOT) 
• 
Preliminary project cost estimate will be provided at this design review 
o 
City review time has been estimated to be completed and returned in 5 working days in 
order to maintain proposed project schedule. 
• 
It is assumed that intersection geometry and horizontal location of proposed new utilities will be 
largely finalized for this plan review.  Minor adjustments to accommodate ROW limitations and/or 
utility conflicts will be established in the 90% Plans submittal. 
 
TASK SERIES 3.0 – Final Design Phase 
 
Task 3.1 – Traffic Engineering, Lighting, Signalization 
 
• 
HDR will analyze traffic volumes to provide guidance and recommendations in determining the 
geometry at the intersection. HDR will review available planning studies (i.e., the Connect 2045 
Cheyenne Area Transportation Master Plan) and how they correlate to the prescribed 
configuration stated in the RFP.  
o 
It is assumed that traffic volumes will be available for use and new counts will not be 
required. 
o 
It is assumed that signal warrants will not be required at currently unsignalized 
intersections. 
• 
HDR will develop traffic signal design, intersection, signing, and striping sheets, and quantities. 
The traffic signal design will be for the 18th Street and Pioneer Ave. intersection and the 18th 
Street and Carey Ave. intersection will be designed to City of Cheyenne standards.  
o 
The signals on Central Ave. and Warren Ave. We assume will not be replaced as 
WYDOT has recently upgraded them. 
• 
The following information will be required to lay out the traffic signal(s) and lighting:  
o 
Existing topography, subsurface utilities and finalized intersection geometrics 
o 
HDR is aware that the City would prefer to reuse the existing signal elements at the 
intersection.  The signal design will be developed to reuse the existing signal components 
if/where possible. 
• 
Signing and striping design will include 18th Street from Snyder Ave. to House Ave  
o 
This work will be in accordance with the MUTCD and the City of Cheyenne Standards 
• 
HDR will with subconsultant Terracon to perform L-Pile analysis for Signal Footing Design based 
on geotechnical parameters 
 
Task 3.2 a&b – Detailed Underground Utility Design (Water, Sewer, Storm Sewer) 
 
• 
HDR will evaluate existing utilities and identify potential conflicts with proposed improvements. 
o 
Public utilities include water, sanitary sewer, and storm sewer 
• 
HDR will update utility design to address comments and advance design for replacement of the 
existing potable water system within the project area, proposed storm sewer interceptor and inlets 
and for replacement sanitary sewer manholes within the project area. 

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming | 18th Street Reconstruction Project
Scope of Services – Rev.2 | May 2025
 
6 
 
o 
The design team will coordinate with Cheyenne BOPU for final design of replacement of 
water mains, fire hydrants & fire hydrant leads and service lines within the ROW of the 
project area.   
o 
The design team will provide final storm sewer design including storm drain interceptor 
alignment and profile and final anticipated placement of inlets.  Storm sewer design will 
be completed following the preliminary design effort and the Grading Plans submittal.  A 
reasonably accurate proposed ground surface will be necessary to depict surface 
drainage and design the collection system.  It is assumed that surface drainage will be 
collected in inlets with lateral piping to upgraded storm sewer ‘mains’ OR connected to 
existing storm sewer ‘main’ line piping OR will mimic the existing condition on-site and 
can be discharged into the adjacent Crow Creek drainage basin with appropriate erosion 
and sediment control measures.  
o 
It is assumed that connection to existing storm sewer will be made without the need for 
additional storm sewer treatment.  
o 
Inlets will be designed to comply with City of Cheyenne design standards 
 
• 
HDR will provide an updated final conditions model for the 18th street updates along with the 
Drainage Impact Study for the City’s review. This study will comply with Section 3.2 of the UDC 
and will assess the following: 
o 
Upstream and downstream impacts 
o 
Compliance with drainage criteria for storm sewer design.  
o 
Development of grading and erosion control permit.  
o 
Final Drainage Plan will be provided at this stage.  
• 
The design team will coordinate with Cheyenne BOPU for final design of replacement sanitary 
sewer manholes within the ROW of the project area.  
• 
Utility design work for water and sanitary sewer will be progressed and communicated with the 
City and the Cheyenne BOPU (with City approval). 
o 
Assumes no water or sanitary sewer modeling will be required (BOPU will provide) 
 
Task 3.3 – H&H Drainage Model Updates 
 
• 
HDR will review the available models and create project- speciﬁc SWMM models that analyze the 
proposed 18th Street storm water system, including inlets, laterals, trunk lines and surface ﬂows.  
o 
This model will be imported into either InfoSWMM or PCSWMM to facilitate scenario 
management and model updates in accordance with the design process. Both 
InfoSWMM and PCSWMM use the SWMM 5 engine and provides seamless import and 
export functions to go between the two modeling platforms, with the final deliverables to 
be provided in the SWMM 5 format.  
• 
HDR will update hydraulic & hydrologic model based on the information gathered in task 2.8. 
o 
This assumes provision of the existing stormwater model for drainage areas that will 
possibly contribute or interact with the project scope along 18th Street 
• 
HDR will refine design and model through the progression of final design  
o 
This includes the final model deliverable reflecting the final design plans 
 
Task 3.4 – Geotechnical Recommendation(s) Final 
 
• 
Geotechnical partner Terracon will finalize Geotechnical Investigation efforts with a final report 
detailing bearing capacities for structural design, surfacing section(s) for use in the intersection & 
roadways, and on-site soil characteristics that will be encountered during construction. 
• 
HDR will review Terracon’s final deliverable(s) for content and applicability

• 
90% Design Plans will be submitted with accompanying information necessary to finalize design 
review(s) and enter the Final Plan preparation phase 
o 
It is assumed that legal survey will not be required, and ROW acquisition has been 
completed by the City prior to submission of these plans. 
• 
 Plans will include drafts for summaries, details of utility adjustments, plan and profile sheets, 
draft set of cross sections, and a 90% set of project special provisions 
• 
City review time must be completed and returned in 5 working days in order to maintain desired

o 
It is assumed that adjustments to public utilities will be completed solely with the City of

o 
No Private Utility Relocations will be designed specifically by HDR.  It is assumed the

Project Plan review will be completed between City, BOPU, and HDR.  Other agency review will

•

o 
This includes summary tables for bid items being encountered in the work 
o 
Cross Section Drawings will be provided for pertinent utility crossing(s) and roadway 
sections 
• 
Project quantities will be taken from the bid form to generate an Engineer’s Estimate  
o 
HDR will follow the American Association of Cost Estimators guidelines during this effort 
• 
Bid items will be based on Technical Specifications (City of Cheyenne & WYDOT) 
o 
The City will provide front-end documents for bidding (i.e., “front-end” documents such as

• 
HDR will provide recommendations to the City on desired milestones and pertinent data such as

o 
HDR will provide project Special Provisions to supplement the Technical Specifications 
o 
City review time must be completed and returned in 5 working days in order to maintain

• 
Hard Copies of Project documents will be provided per the Scope of Work in the RFP 
o 
Project will go through the HDR QC process for Quality Control prior to bidding.  
o 
Estimated Total Plan Sheet Count of 80 pages including Title Page, Summaries, P&P 
Sheets, Utility Plans, and Standard Details

Cross Sections will be provided on 50-foot intervals (Est. 20 pages)

Final Deliverable will include

• 
Quality Assurance/Quality Control Review 
o 
HDR will review plans, documents, specifications, for accuracy and reasonableness. 
o 
QA/QC comments, responses, and resolutions for drawings and special provisions will be

TASK SERIES 4.0 – Specialty Services 
 
Task 4.1 – Pre-Construction Structural Existing Condition Assessment 
 
• 
HDR will perform an existing condition assessment of the buildings along the 18th Street Corridor. 
o 
Existing condition assessment will not evaluate structural competence 
o 
Condition assessment will be done with two HDR staff, one will be a Wyoming licensed 
Professional Engineer.  
o 
Effort is anticipated to take approximately 2 weeks of field time followed by 2 weeks of 
documentation and reporting time. 
o 
Condition assessment will be limited to structure exterior(s) only 
• 
Final Deliverables will include: 
o 
Digital Files (photos and/or videos) of existing conditions 
o 
Technical memorandum/report summarizing existing conditions 
 
Task 4.2 – Cultural Resources Review 
 
• 
The following tasks outline HDR’s approach to preparing the project for potential federal funding 
by conducting a resource review that can be utilized in future National Environmental Policy Act 
(NEPA) documentation. While no federal funding is currently secured, this effort aligns reviewed 
data with NEPA process requirements should federal funds be pursued.  
 
• 
Cultural/Environmental Resource Review Process: HDR proposes a two-step process: 1) 
Resources Overview Report – This document will summarize the environmental conditions within 
the study area and support early decision-making. 2) NEPA Document (if needed) – If federal 
funds are pursued, HDR can coordinate with the federal lead agency to determine the appropriate 
NEPA pathway and prepare the required documentation under an additional scope. 
  
• 
Study Area and Methodology: HDR will define a study area extending 500 feet from the project 
centerline for environmental review and approval. This area will form the basis for desktop and 
field studies, ensuring that findings are adequate for future NEPA compliance. The study area 
review will be documented in a Resources Overview Report to inform project planning and 
potential federal review.  
 
• 
Resource Categories to be Reviewed:  
o 
Aquatic Resources and Floodplains: HDR will review publicly available online resources, 
including the National Wetlands Mapper, National Hydrography Dataset (NHD) maps 
through NREX, and Federal Emergency Management Agency (FEMA) floodplain data, to 
determine the presence and extent of water resources and floodplains in the project area. 

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming | 18th Street Reconstruction Project
Scope of Services – Rev.2 | May 2025
 
9 
 
If any regulated aquatic features are identified, HDR will coordinate with the appropriate 
agencies to confirm jurisdictional status and permitting requirements.   
o 
Recreation Properties: Section 4(f) and 6(f) properties within the study area will be 
identified and documented. No additional coordination is anticipated unless required by 
federal agencies.  
o 
Cultural Resources:  A Historic Built Environment Survey for the 18th Street Project: HDR 
is providing design and consulting services to the City of Cheyenne for planned 
improvements to 18th Street (Project). In the event the proposed Project is supported 
through a Federal Highways Administration (FHWA) grant, a federal nexus will be 
established and compliance with Section 106 of the National Historic Preservation Act of 
1966 (NHPA), as amended, and its implementing regulations at 36 Code of Federal 
Regulations §800, will be required. HDR will provide cultural resource support for NHPA 
compliance as part of its consulting and design services for the Project. Associated tasks 
will include completion of a historic built environment survey and preparation of a Section 
106 determination of eligibility and effects analysis report. The report will include 
recommendations for National Register of Historic Places (NRHP) eligibility for cultural 
resources within the Area of Potential Effects (APE) and management recommendations 
for identified historic properties. The survey report will be conducted in accordance with 
Wyoming State Historic Preservation Office (SHPO) standards.  
 
Built Environment Survey: HDR will complete an architectural history survey for 
the built environment (buildings, structures, objects) within the proposed APE. It 
is presumed that the APE will consist of the entire extent of 18th Street within the 
study area and all parcels adjacent to 18th Street (one parcel deep on both sides 
of the street) from Snyder Ave. to House Ave. Built environment resources 
encountered during this review will be evaluated for NRHP eligibility by staff 
meeting the SOI Professional Qualification Standards for history or architectural 
history. The review of built environment resources will include a field survey of 
the direct APE, as well as a review of assessor records, historical aerial imagery, 
and historical maps, to identify structures and buildings which are 50 years or 
older.  
 
The results of the field survey will be presented in a determination of eligibility 
and effects analysis survey report. The report will be prepared in compliance with 
SHPO guidelines for Class III reports (Format, Guidelines, and Standards for 
Class II and Class III Reports [v. 10/2012]). Forms for cultural resources 
documented during the survey will be submitted via WyoTrack and provided in a 
report appendix.  
o 
Biological Resources: A desktop survey will be conducted to assess habitat for federally 
listed threatened and endangered species. U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service’s (USFWS) 
Information for Planning and Consultation (IPaC) tool will be used to generate a species 
list and conduct a determination key analysis as well as a search of the Wyoming Natural 
Diversity Database for Species of Greatest Conservation Need. No species-specific or 
protocol surveys (e.g., for migratory birds, bats, or other special-status species) are 
included in this scope but may be added if required.  
o 
Hazardous Materials: A desktop survey of publicly available data will be conducted to 
determine the presence or absence of hazardous materials sites such as brownfields, 
underground storage tanks (USTs), and other known contaminated sites. HDR will also 
coordinate with the Wyoming Department of Environmental Quality (WDEQ) to identify 
potential environmental concerns specific to the project vicinity.  
o 
Economic Resources: A desktop review of publicly available socioeconomic data will be 
conducted to assess potential impacts on local businesses and overall community 
cohesion. HDR will evaluate potential disruptions to commercial activity, access to 
services, and neighborhood connectivity.  
• 
Deliverables  
o 
Draft and final Resources Overview Report, including:  
o 
GIS-generated study area map  
o 
WY SHPO WyoTrack record search results  
o 
USFWS IPaC species list  

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming | 18th Street Reconstruction Project
Scope of Services – Rev.2 | May 2025
 
10 
 
o 
Agency correspondence and summary  
• 
HDR Resources Overview Assumptions  
o 
The study area will not exceed the initially defined limits.  
o 
Archival research (e.g., General Land Office maps, historical maps, Sanborn Fire 
Insurance maps, assessor records) is not included in this scope.  
o 
HDR will draft up to three agency coordination letters and summarize responses from 
those agencies in the Resources Overview Report.  
o 
No aquatic resources (wetlands or Waters of the U.S.) are present in the project area, so 
a 404 Permit Application is not required.  
o 
The Resources Overview Report will summarize the findings but will not constitute a 
NEPA decision.  
o 
If federal funding is acquired, the appropriate class of NEPA documentation will be 
determined under a separate scope amendment.  
o 
Changes to the general project alignment may necessitate an amendment to this scope 
of services.  
o 
HDR Cultural Resources Assumptions  
o 
HDR will record and evaluate built environment resources which are 50 years or older.  
o 
HDR assumes that any right-of-way (ROW) acquisitions requiring survey will be identified 
prior to the commencement of field surveys.  
o 
HDR assumes right-of-entry to all survey areas will be provided prior to the start of 
fieldwork.  
o 
HDR assumes no significant delays after mobilization due to weather or activities beyond 
the control of HDR. In the event of such delays labor will be invoiced as time-and-
materials and expenses will be invoiced as accrued.  
o 
Should the project have an adverse effect on NRHP-eligible or listed structures, 
buildings, objects, or historic districts identified during the survey, HDR may require 
additional funding for consultation with WYDOT and SHPO to determine and conduct 
appropriate mitigation measures.  
o 
HDR assumes one review of each of the draft, draft final, and final cultural resource 
report and site forms will be conducted by WYDOT prior to submittal to SHPO.   
o 
HDR will not record or consider potential adverse effects to property types listed as 
defined non-sites within the SHPO guidelines (Resource Definitions and Recording 
Guidelines [v. 9/2012]).  
o 
HDR assumes only parcels which directly overlap the APE will require survey and that full 
surveys of entire historic districts will not be required.  
o 
HDR anticipates assessing effects to two NRHP-listed historic districts, and NRHP 
evaluation and effects analysis for up to 40 privately owned parcels with buildings 50 
years of age or older that overlap the APE and one linear railroad segment that crosses 
the APE.    
o 
Up to 4 virtual meetings with the SHPO and/or Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board

•

o
